Despite the decisions made, the media also found that the shoes were not destroyed, but were put on the market. In order to take all necessary measures against these cancer-maker products, including the collection of shoes from the market that cause great doubt and anxiety, we would like to submit a parliamentary investigation into the Constitution under Articles 104 and 105 of the Constitution's 98th and TEMM Institution. 2/3/2016 In August 2014, carcinogens, known as azore dye, were found in imported shoes held in the foyer of a company operating under the İstanbul Erenköy Customs Directorate, and were not allowed to import it due to about five truckloads of 25,510 carcinogens in Chinese origins. Under procedure, the removal of the shoes was initiated, and the necessary assessments by Customs Directorate were made on August 24, 2014, and the shoes were re-loaded into trucks to be sent to the Disposal Facility for disposal. Loaded shoes for trucks were brought to EAYDAŞ facilities on October 20, 2014, under security. Customs officials who came to the facility under the approval required to dispose of the shoes containing carcinogens have discovered that the boxes they opened were not real shoes that were decided to destroy the shoes they encountered, but used, used, and torn shoes that were returned from the market. After a thorough examination of the surveying team, the old and torn shoes were found to be the same brand of shoes that were imported. After this determination, the arbitraryness was reported to the authorities in both Istanbul customs and Ankara, and the trucks were relocated to Erenköy Customs Directorate, not being processed for the cargo of trucks. The analysis of this frame found no trace of the shoes containing cancerous substances, including children's shoes. However, a search of the trucks found several city-owned product return forms among the items. The study also found that the shoes imported could not be obtained from TSE for carrying carcinogens. In addition to the discovery of the incident, an examination of the warehouse where the shoes were kept during the process has been launched, and according to the Foreign Trade Undersecretary's statement, the product carrying azoor paint has been found to be among the products that are banned from importing. Customs and Trade Minister Nurettin Canikli confirmed that thousands of toxic shoes had been put on the market that day, and stated that it seemed sold to the domestic market, and that it was not known how much of it had been put on the market. The minister's words have caused a great deal of panic.
